14:I[8669,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DateSidebar"]
15:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
16:T647,The system starts from frozen document embeddings with dimension 1024 over about 5×10^7 documents. HERMES first applies a Learned Semantic Transform, h_i = normalize(W e_i + b), with W initialized as the identity. The transform is trained to make the embedding space more quantization-friendly while preserving semantic structure. The losses include pairwise structure preservation, quantization-aware reconstruction, and an orthogonality constraint to avoid degenerate rotations. A three-stage residual vector quantizer then emits a code (c1, c2, c3), with codebook size K=256 at every stage. The first stage gives L1 buckets, the first two stages give L12 buckets, and all three stages give L123 buckets. In the observed corpus this corresponds to 256 buckets, roughly 65k buckets, and roughly 130k cells. Since all resolutions come from the same trained codebook stack, changes in downstream behavior can be attributed to granularity and sampling rules rather than to different reclustering runs. For training mixtures, the outer weights are kept at the L1 level using Uniform or DoReMi, which avoids fitting a high-dimensional mixer over tens of thousands of cells. The Stage-2 sampler controls which documents are drawn within the coarse buckets. The key comparison is between max-entropy coverage, which spreads mass across sub-buckets and keeps all documents eligible, and quality top-30%, which uses a corrected FineWeb-Edu reader to select the top 30% within the relevant candidate sets. A quota-preserving L123 comparison is added to isolate the effect of candidate competition under finer cells.17:T45a,The downstream study uses a 1B-parameter model trained for 25B tokens and evaluates a 16-task capability macro-average. At K=256, HERMES is on the same plateau as four standard 256-way clusterers on compactness and mass-balance metrics. It is also essentially matched to KMeans in downstream performance, within 0.0002 Avg. The main controlled experiment is at L12 under fixed corpus, model, codebook, outer weight, and granularity. With DoReMi-L1 outer weights, switching Stage-2 from max-entropy coverage to quality top-30% improves Avg. by +0.0253, with z=+2.09. The finer-granularity result changes the story. Under a quota-preserving L123 setup, per-L1 local random coverage scores 0.3986 and L123 quality top-30% scores 0.3988, a difference of only +0.0002 under single-seed evaluation. The proposed explanation is candidate competition: the median candidate pool shrinks from 2,271 documents at L12 to 429 at L123, a 5.3× reduction. Once the candidate set becomes small, within-cell quality ranking becomes a weaker proxy for global document quality. This is the strongest experimental insight in the work.18:T4c0,First, a unified theoretical perspective is established: the second derivative of cross-entropy loss equals the squared gradient (Fisher information metric, the only canonical local geometry of KL divergence), and all high-performing Transformer optimizers effectively condition gradients by the square root of this metric, equivalent to dividing by the estimated standard deviation of gradient estimates. Ember is built on two core design choices: 1) Remove Adam's first-moment EMA entirely and use instantaneous gradients — ablation shows this change improves performance while saving V×D memory; 2) Leverage the observation that row-wise gradient energy of token interfaces is an unbiased estimator of token occurrence probability p_i, and row-wise Fisher scales with p_i², so full V×D second-moment buffers are unnecessary. Only V row-wise and D column-wise second-moment EMAs are maintained to approximate curvature. Key differences from prior work: Adafactor uses similar second-moment factorization but has more hyperparameters and is not purpose-built for token interface geometry; Muon only applies to linear layers and relies on singular value decomposition, making it unsuitable for embedding tables.19:T407,Proposes Mixture-of-Parallelisms (MoP), a training stack that abandons uniform global parallel plans and assigns sharding strategies matched to each MoE component's dominant bottleneck. All composed parallelism methods maintain a data-parallel-like rank invariant (each device holds a distinct token shard and corresponding weight shard), avoiding the rank multiplication overhead of 3D parallelism. Three core lossless optimizations are included: 1) A memory-efficient expert parallelism built on Least-Loaded Expert Parallelism (LLEP) that overlaps routing communication with expert computation to reduce transient MoE activation footprint while preserving LLEP speedups; 2) A sharded data-tensor-parallel vocabulary projection that computes exact loss and gradients without materializing the O(NV) logit tensor, while respecting the data-parallel rank invariant; 3) A pipelined optimizer update scheme that compresses critical path time via computation-communication overlap. All optimizations preserve exact training semantics.1a:T491,The design relies on two observations. First, in 3D parallel training, model shards and optimizer shards have different recoverability properties. Model parameters are often replicated across data-parallel peers and can be reconstructed from healthy ranks with the same logical partition. Sharded optimizer states, including first and second moments, are distributed within a data-parallel rank and may be lost when a node fails permanently. DeadPool therefore replicates optimizer-state shards to neighboring nodes in memory at every iteration, using asynchronous communication overlapped with compute to keep the backup off the critical path. Second, recovery is handled as a topology-repair problem. A spare node replaces the failed node, communication groups are reconstructed at runtime, and training resumes without terminating the whole job. The system also classifies failures so that topology-changing recovery is only used when node membership is truly invalidated. The implementation is built on PyTorch and Megatron-LM, but the required abstractions are generic: sharded states, spare-node management, recovery coordination, and communicator reconstruction.1b:Ta9b,方法上，PAW 由三个部件组成：伪程序生成器、LoRA 编译器、冻结解释器。整体流程不是让一个模型直接根据规格输出答案，而是先把规格整理成更稳定的中间表示，再生成任务专属参数，最后由小模型执行。

第一步是 pseudo compiler。它使用一个未训练的 4B Qwen3，通过固定模板把用户规格改写成 pseudo-program。这个 pseudo-program 由两部分组成：一段更清晰、去歧义的任务重述，以及少量输入输出示例。这样做的原因很直接：原始用户描述常常含糊、冗余、带 typo，直接拿来生成权重不稳定；先做一次离散化重写，相当于把自然语言规格投影到更规整的任务空间。

第二步是 LoRA compiler。这是论文真正训练的核心模型，也是一个 4B Qwen3 级别编译器。它读取原始规格和 pseudo-program，然后输出一组可注入解释器的 LoRA 参数。论文把这一步看成“从任务描述到神经二进制”的编译。相比前作用 prefix-tuning KV cache 作为程序表示，这一版改用 text-to-LoRA。作者明确比较后认为 LoRA 更强，因为它不是只在输入侧加条件，而是直接改变解释器内部线性层的行为，控制粒度更细，容量也更高。

第三步是 interpreter。解释器是冻结的小模型，主实验用 Qwen3-0.6B。运行时输入包括用户样本 x、pseudo-program 的离散部分，以及编译得到的 LoRA。也就是说，程序是“离散 token + 连续参数”的混合体。作者强调这个混合设计不是实现细节，而是关键抽象：离散部分负责鲁棒地表达任务语义，连续部分负责补足文本无法精确指定的行为边界。

训练数据方面，论文发布了 FuzzyBench，规模 1000 万样本，分 29 个主题版本，覆盖 800 多类模糊文本任务，包括分类、格式转换、解析、模糊匹配、自然语言命令、agent 工具使用等。数据是逐步扩展构建的，目标不是单一 benchmark，而是覆盖“可由自然语言规格定义、但难以用规则精确实现”的函数空间。这个数据设计很关键，因为编译器学的不是某个任务，而是从规格泛化到新任务的映射。

从建模角度看，PAW 和直接 instruction tuning 的区别在于：它不把所有任务都压进一个统一前向过程，而是显式生成任务特定权重；和元学习或 ICL 的区别在于：任务适配结果被固化成可保存、可版本化的程序文件；和普通 LoRA 微调的区别在于：LoRA 不是通过梯度优化得到，而是由编译器一次前向生成。这使它更像 hypernetwork 驱动的条件参数化系统。1c:T4eb,Its value for pretraining research is mostly architectural and paradigm-level. PAW reframes weights as a program representation, not just a repository of general capability. Instead of paying inference-time reasoning cost on every input, it shifts substantial work to compile time: a larger compiler maps a task spec into a small task-specific adapter, and a lightweight interpreter executes it repeatedly. That is a meaningful alternative to the standard autoregressive pattern of “one general model, one prompt, one forward pass per request.” The reported result that a 0.6B interpreter plus compiled adapters can match or exceed direct prompting of a 32B model suggests that some capability currently expressed as online contextual reasoning can be externalized into offline task specialization. For pretraining, this opens several directions: PEFT modules as executable intermediate representations, datasets for spec-to-weight compilation rather than only next-token prediction, and a cleaner separation between universal pretrained competence and compiled task-local behavior. It also connects naturally to hypernetworks, conditional weight generation, and the question of when in-context learning can be converted into persistent parameter updates.1d:Tb25,PAW consists of three components: a pseudo-compiler, a LoRA compiler, and a frozen interpreter. The system is not just prompting a model with a better template; it explicitly compiles a task into a hybrid neural program.

Stage 1 is the pseudo-compiler. An off-the-shelf 4B Qwen3 model, kept untrained, rewrites the user specification into a pseudo-program. This pseudo-program contains a cleaned task restatement plus a handful of input-output examples. The motivation is practical: raw user specs are ambiguous, verbose, typo-prone, and often unstable as direct conditioning signals. The rewrite step creates a more canonical discrete representation of the task before any weight generation happens.

Stage 2 is the LoRA compiler, the main trained component. It is also built on a 4B Qwen3-scale model. Given the original spec and the pseudo-program, it emits a LoRA adapter to be injected into the interpreter. This is the actual “program binary.” The paper positions this as a shift from text-only task conditioning to generated parameterization. Compared with the authors’ earlier prefix-tuning-based precursor, the current version uses text-to-LoRA and reports better performance. That choice matters: prefix tuning mainly conditions through the input side, while LoRA changes internal linear transformations of the interpreter and therefore offers finer control and more representational capacity.

Stage 3 is the interpreter, a frozen small LM, with Qwen3-0.6B as the main instantiation. At execution time, the interpreter receives the input x, the discrete pseudo-program tokens, and the compiled LoRA. So the program is explicitly hybrid: a discrete component for semantic anchoring and a continuous component for behavioral precision. The paper argues that this split is not incidental. The discrete part protects against ambiguity in the original spec, while the continuous part captures nuances that text alone cannot pin down reliably.

Training relies on FuzzyBench, a 10M-example dataset released with the paper. It is built incrementally across 29 thematic versions and covers more than 800 categories of fuzzy text tasks, including classification, format conversion, parsing, fuzzy matching, natural-language commands, and agentic tool use. This dataset is central because the compiler is not learning one task family; it is learning a mapping from task descriptions to executable task-specific parameterizations.

Conceptually, PAW differs from standard instruction tuning because it does not force all tasks through a single shared forward policy. It differs from ordinary LoRA finetuning because the adapter is not obtained by gradient descent per task, but generated in one shot by a compiler. And it differs from pure in-context learning because the adaptation result is persistent, versionable, and distributable as a file.1e:T80d,The headline result is the main accuracy-cost tradeoff. The paper reports that a Qwen3-0.6B interpreter running PAW programs reaches 73.78% exact match, outperforming direct prompting of Qwen3-32B at 68.70%, while using roughly one fiftieth of the inference memory. That is the key empirical claim: for this family of fuzzy functions, offline task compilation can be more effective than repeatedly invoking a much larger general model.

Deployment numbers are also concrete. In quantized form, the shared interpreter base is about 430MB in GGUF format, and each compiled program is about 23MB as a LoRA adapter. The system runs at around 30 tokens per second on a MacBook M3. The paper also describes a smaller GPT-2-based path that runs fully client-side in the browser via WebAssembly. These details matter because the work is making a systems claim about software packaging and local execution, not only a modeling claim.

The case studies cover output triage, custom classification, fuzzy search reranking, agent preprocessing, and creative generation. The agent preprocessing setup reaches 93% on ToolCall-15. The point is not a single benchmark win, but evidence that PAW is not limited to narrow label classification; it can also support tasks with tool-selection and preprocessing structure.

The paper further shows modality generality: by replacing only the compiler with a vision-language model and keeping the same interpreter, PAW can handle image-conditioned fuzzy tasks. That suggests the executable representation is not tied to text-only specifications; cross-modal understanding can be absorbed by the compiler while execution remains unified.

On ablations, the abstract already states that LoRA outperforms prefix-tuning as the program form. The most important experimental signal is not the absolute score itself, but the fact that a frozen 0.6B interpreter plus generated task weights can cross the performance level of direct prompting on a 32B model. That strongly supports the paper’s central thesis about compile-time specialization.1f:T6b5,Start with the introduction and the formal definition of Programs as Weights. The key thing to internalize is that the “program” here is neither a prompt nor a finetuned checkpoint in the usual sense. It is a hybrid executable object: pseudo-program tokens plus PEFT parameters. If that abstraction is missed, the rest can look like ordinary adapter generation.

Then read the compile pipeline carefully. Focus on why the pseudo-compiler is left untrained, why the LoRA compiler is trained separately, and why the interpreter is frozen. Those design boundaries reveal the authors’ decomposition of the problem into semantic normalization, parameter synthesis, and execution.

In the experiments, prioritize the main comparison tables against direct prompting, model scale, and alternative program representations. The 0.6B interpreter versus 32B direct prompting result is the central validation point. After that, read the case studies to judge whether the method covers a genuine class of software functions rather than a narrow benchmark slice.

For deeper study, four questions are worth tracking. First, whether FuzzyBench adequately represents the real distribution of fuzzy functions or contains templated biases. Second, whether generated LoRAs have interpretable structure, for example clustering by task family in a low-rank subspace. Third, how much responsibility is carried by the pseudo-program versus the continuous adapter. Fourth, where compile-time specialization stops being enough and online reasoning is still necessary, such as long-horizon reasoning, open-ended generation, or strong compositional generalization. Reading with those questions in mind will make the paper much more useful.20:T71b,Wiola is still a decoder-only autoregressive LM, but each layer is modified in five places. First, WiolaRMSNorm adds a learned per-dimension offset before RMS normalization. The motivation is that pure rescaling cannot shift the effective zero point of the representation, so it may be insufficient against deep-layer collapse into a low-rank subspace. The overhead is tiny, and RMSNorm is recovered when the offset is zero. Second, SRPE extends RoPE-style 2D rotations into a helical 3D positional construction with a radial term. The goal is to encode token-, phrase-, and discourse-scale structure in one analytic form. Unlike standard RoPE, which is purely relative in the phase term, SRPE keeps relative-offset behavior while introducing controlled absolute-position dependence through the radial product. Third, GCLA gives each layer gated access to compressed summaries from the previous two layers. The summaries are mean pooled, and the gate is initialized near zero, so the model starts close to a standard stack and only learns to use cross-layer context when beneficial. Fourth, ATM performs dynamic greedy merging of semantically redundant adjacent tokens in the middle third of layers, then restores sequence length later so the external interface remains unchanged. The placement is deliberate: early merging risks damaging local pattern formation, while late merging can interfere with output alignment. Fifth, DSFF replaces a single FFN path with dual parallel streams intended to separate local and global feature extraction, followed by learned per-dimension fusion. Overall, the architecture tries to distribute responsibilities: SRPE for multi-scale geometry, GCLA for layer-to-layer memory, ATM for mid-layer compression, DSFF for feature specialization, and WiolaRMSNorm for deep-stack stability.21:T449,The strongest quantitative details in the provided text are about overhead, not benchmark strength. The paper specifies a wiola-360m configuration. WiolaRMSNorm adds only 2Ld parameters over RMSNorm; for the 360M model this is 32,768 parameters, or 0.009% of the total. GCLA adds 2BTΛHd_h FLOPs per layer, which the authors estimate as roughly Λ/T of self-attention cost; with T=2048 and Λ=2, that is about 0.1%, effectively negligible. ATM is claimed to reduce attention FLOPs by 5–9% during training. For SRPE, the comparison to RoPE is mostly analytical: it preserves a relative-position term while adding absolute-position sensitivity through the radial component. The main limitation is that the provided full content does not expose enough of the core empirical section: no clear perplexity table, no strong same-scale baselines, no full ablation across the five modules, and no detailed stability curves under different context lengths or merge ratios. So the current evidence supports low overhead and coherent design intent, but not yet robust claims of consistent pretraining gains.22:T466,HOLA (Hippocampal Linear Attention) implements a semiparametric test-time memory with two complementary components: the standard delta-rule recurrent state, which acts as a parametric compressor for linearly generalizable structure (analogous to neocortical memory); and a bounded-capacity exact KV cache, which acts as a non-parametric episodic store for one-shot associations that cannot be well absorbed into the compressed state (analogous to hippocampal memory). For cache writes, no learned eviction module is required: the model uses the intrinsic surprise signal already computed during delta rule updates -- the magnitude of the committed residual correction to the state β·||e|| -- as the eviction score, retaining tokens that produce large state updates rather than holding only recent tokens as in sliding window approaches. For cache reads, a dedicated RMSNorm-γ scaling is applied exclusively to the cache path to fix the overly uniform softmax issue caused by low-magnitude logits from unit-norm keys, enabling sharp near-argmax exact retrieval without destabilizing the delta rule's recurrent state updates.23:T808,The method, PartRep, performs selective repetition instead of duplicating the entire prompt. Given an input sequence t, it chooses a subsequence t_part and appends only that subsequence near the end of the prompt. The goal is to create late-position echoes for the most informative tokens while staying within a repetition budget τ.

The formulation is deliberately constrained: the selector must output a subsequence of the original prompt, not a summary or a rewritten version. This keeps the selector lightweight, since it only needs to make binary keep/drop decisions per token. A more general compressor would require generation in vocabulary space and would likely erase the efficiency advantage.

For token importance, the paper uses token-wise negative log-likelihood as the main signal. The intuition is simple: high-NLL tokens are hard to predict from the preceding context, so their information is not redundantly encoded elsewhere. Repeating them should therefore provide the largest marginal gain when placed later in the sequence. The paper argues, both conceptually and empirically, that repeating high-surprisal tokens is more useful than repeating low-surprisal or random tokens.

Exact NLL, however, would require a full forward pass, which defeats the purpose. To avoid that, the authors train a lightweight gate that predicts the top-τ high-NLL tokens from early-layer hidden states of the target model. The gate is implemented as a small two-layer MLP with attention on top of intermediate representations, enabling token selection during mid-prefill. The selected tokens are then appended through a short natural-language bridge, and the model performs a single final generation pass.

Mechanistically, PartRep does not change model parameters and is not a retrieval method. It exploits a known property of autoregressive transformers: tokens placed later in the sequence are more accessible under causal attention. The key claim is that prompt information is highly non-uniform, so only a small subset of tokens needs to be echoed.24:T595,The paper evaluates on 8 benchmarks, including GSM8K and RULER, and across several decoder-only model families such as Qwen2.5, Llama3.2, and Gemma4. The main result is that PartRep largely preserves the accuracy gains of full repetition, and in some settings slightly exceeds it, while using only 59.4% of the KV cache budget and 79.0% of the prefill FLOPs. This is the central empirical point: the benefit of repetition does not require duplicating the whole prompt.

The gains appear on tasks that require stronger context integration or long-context access, which is consistent with the method’s motivation. The paper also studies token-selection strategies to validate NLL as an importance signal. Even though the provided excerpt does not include all tables, the method description makes clear that the authors compare oracle-like NLL selection, learned gate selection, and weaker baselines such as random or heuristic choices. The important takeaway is not the exact score gap, but the pattern: true NLL ranking is a strong selector, and the lightweight gate is accurate enough to recover most of that benefit without paying the full cost.

Another strength is that the paper treats efficiency as a first-class metric. It reports not only task accuracy but also prefill FLOPs and KV cache footprint. That makes PartRep more than a prompt trick; it is an inference-time systems method targeted at long-context deployment.25:T7b6,The central intuition comes from teacher forcing. In an autoregressive drafter, the loss at position i is naturally conditioned on a gold prefix on the input side, so supervising later positions makes sense only under the event that earlier positions are already correct. A mask-only block drafter has no such input-side conditioning channel because all block positions are predicted in parallel. AUF approximates that missing prefix conditioning on the loss side.

Concretely, the drafter predicts a block of length B in parallel. The method then scans from left to right, finds the first prediction error, and keeps gold cross-entropy only up to that point. The accepted prefix and the first failing token remain supervised; all later positions are masked out of the loss. Keeping the first failing token is important because throughput depends not only on accepted-prefix length but also on correcting the earliest mismatch that breaks verification.

The paper positions AUF against two existing families. One is DFlash-style full-block masked CE with a hand-designed positional decay prior, which encodes the heuristic that earlier positions matter more. The other is acceptance-aware training such as GRIFFIN and D-PACE: GRIFFIN truncates or downweights based on Top-K prefix criteria, while D-PACE keeps full-block support and uses soft weights to approximate prefix conditioning. AUF differs in two ways: it uses exact first-error truncation rather than a coarse Top-K proxy, and it changes the support of the loss rather than merely reweighting all positions.

Methodologically, AUF is attractive because it is simple, architecture-light, and removes a tuned decay hyperparameter. The obvious trade-off is sparser supervision, especially early in training when first errors occur near the beginning of the block. While reading, it is worth checking whether the paper discusses warmup behavior, curriculum effects, or optimization stability under this sparser signal.26:T6e1,The main empirical claim is straightforward: under a Qwen3-8B target setup, AUF improves the acceptance-length metric τ over the decayed-CE DFlash baseline on all six evaluated benchmarks. The paper also reports that the gain transfers from DFlash to Domino’s two-branch head, which suggests the effect is not tied to one specific drafter architecture.

A particularly important result is that once AUF is used on DFlash, retaining the original exponential positional decay within the truncated support does not further improve τ on any of the six benchmarks. This directly supports the paper’s thesis: what matters is where supervision stops, not a manually chosen front-heavy weighting curve over the whole block. In other words, AUF makes the hand-designed positional prior redundant in the tested setting.

The paper also contrasts AUF with a GRIFFIN-style Top-3 prefix mask. Prior work had reported that such a baseline underperforms decayed CE on block drafters. The authors argue that this does not invalidate prefix-aware supervision itself; rather, Top-K membership is an AR-oriented criterion that transfers poorly to mask-only block drafting. Exact first-error truncation works better in this setting. That is a useful negative-to-positive comparison because it shows that acceptance-aware objectives do not transfer mechanically from AR drafters to parallel block drafters.

The excerpt does not include the full numeric tables, so the exact margins, block sizes, training budgets, and wall-clock trade-offs should be checked in the main results section. The most important details to extract are absolute and relative τ gains, consistency across benchmarks, any effect on target-equivalent correctness, and whether convergence speed changes.27:T4c8,Start with the introduction and preliminaries. Make sure the verifier’s acceptance rule, the implementation-specific definition τ = A + 1, and the DFlash train–inference mismatch are fully clear. Once that is in place, the motivation for AUF becomes almost self-evident.

Then read the method section carefully. Three details matter: whether the first error is defined by argmax or sampled predictions, why the first failing token is still supervised, and whether the loss is normalized to account for the varying number of active positions after truncation.

In the experiments, first inspect the main comparison table, then the ablations. The most informative ablation is the one showing that AUF plus positional decay is essentially the same as AUF alone, because that is the strongest evidence that support truncation, not hand-tuned weighting, is doing the work. The transfer result to Domino is the next thing to check, since it speaks to generality.

For a quick decision on whether the idea is worth trying, the method section plus the main tables should take about 20 minutes. For actual deployment, the appendices on implementation details, training stability, and block-size sensitivity are likely necessary.28:T466,ReContext is a training-agnostic multi-round inference pipeline that keeps the full original context in the prompt at all times, and does not modify attention logits during final decoding. The process runs a fixed small number of recursive evidence selection rounds: in each round, attention scores are computed on a prompt that includes the evidence pool accumulated in prior rounds. Scores are aggregated across selected attention heads, from the last w=8 cue tokens near the query. High-relevance token spans from the original context are added to an ordered evidence pool, which is inserted into the prompt for the next round. Because attention is computed from a model state that already contains prior selected evidence, later rounds can surface multi-hop information related to previously found spans. At final generation, the model produces answers from a prompt containing the full original context, the recursive evidence pool, and the query. The method is formalized under an associative memory framework, with a monotonicity proof showing evidence replay shifts hidden states closer to the target answer embedding.29:T7f8,The proposed method is JSTIP, Joint Speech-Text Interleaved Pretraining. The architecture remains standard: a pretrained speech encoder, an adapter into the LLM embedding space, and a decoder-only LLM. The novelty is entirely in how training examples are constructed.

Given an aligned speech-text pair (A, T), the authors first obtain fine-grained alignments {(A1, T1), ..., (An, Tn)}. Instead of feeding the full speech as conditioning context and predicting the full transcript, they build interleaved sequences that alternate speech and text segments. Two complementary deterministic templates are used: one starts with speech, e.g. (A1, T2, A3, T4, ..., Tn), and the other starts with text, e.g. (T1, A2, T3, A4, ..., Tn). In both cases, the sequence ends with text, and cross-entropy is applied only on text tokens; all speech positions are masked out from the loss.

Three design choices matter. First, interleaving granularity: the paper studies both word-level and segment-level alignment. Word-level interleaving is more aggressive in coupling speech evidence with local text continuation, while segment-level interleaving is simpler and likely more stable. Second, behavioral consistency: ASR-only, text-only, and interleaved examples are all cast into the same “predict text tokens from a mixed prefix” format, which helps preserve the original LM behavior instead of letting speech-conditioned decoding drift into a separate mode. Third, modality-gap reduction: speech and text no longer coexist only across batches; they co-occur within the same sequence, so the model repeatedly transitions between modalities inside a single causal context.

The authors intentionally use deterministic alternation rather than random modality selection. This is a controlled design choice to isolate the effect of interleaving granularity and boundary construction. On the implementation side, they pack multiple examples into an 8k context and use packed SFT with cu_seqlens in FlashAttention so attention is reset at sample boundaries.2a:T70a,The main experiments use 38k hours of in-house ASR data. The central question is whether JSTIP transfers textual knowledge into ASR more effectively than ASR-only training or naive speech-text joint training. The headline result reported in the paper is up to 17.2% relative improvement in entity accuracy over ASR-only training. This is an important choice of metric: the gains are concentrated on entities and domain-specific terms, which is exactly where one would expect pretrained text priors to matter.

A particularly informative result comes from the domain adaptation setting. The paper compares two ways of injecting domain knowledge: adding transcription-only domain text versus using synthetic domain ASR data. Under JSTIP, domain text alone reaches entity performance comparable to synthetic domain ASR. This suggests that the interleaving scheme materially improves text-to-ASR transfer efficiency, reducing dependence on expensive speech synthesis or additional speech supervision.

The paper also reports that the best JSTIP system is competitive with open-source ASR and Speech-LLM systems on medical entity performance. Another useful signal is zero-shot speech question answering: JSTIP enables this capability without extra task-specific supervision. The authors do not position SQA as the main task, but as evidence that text-side generative behavior survives the ASR-oriented training.

The most useful ablation-level takeaway is the causal chain established by the experiments: ASR-only training increasingly specializes toward speech-conditioned decoding; simple joint training adds text batches but does not transfer much of that knowledge into ASR; interleaving speech and text within the same sequence improves both entity recognition and preservation of generative behavior.2b:T471,The teacher supervision signal is decomposed into two separate components: a reference-induced component that activates even when the teacher only sees the gold answer, which drives rote memorization of reference-specific reasoning shortcuts and is non-transferable; and a question-conditioned component that depends on the problem context, which carries transferable reasoning corrections and is the only useful training signal. Measurements across training checkpoints show the reference-induced component dominates both the direction and magnitude of parameter updates, while the useful transferable component is often nearly orthogonal or even anti-aligned with the total update. The purified distillation target is constructed via a pointwise mutual information adjustment: subtracting the log-probability of a reference-only teacher (conditioned solely on the answer, not the question) from the full teacher log-probability to strip shortcut signals, leaving only transferable corrections. This target directly replaces the raw teacher distribution in standard OPSD, with a single hyperparameter β controlling correction strength.2c:T506,First, any policy weight is decomposed into positive gradient mass m_S (contribution from correct trajectories) and negative gradient mass m_F (contribution from incorrect trajectories), both functions of a prompt's solve rate p. This decomposition reveals three orthogonal design axes: the difficulty axis (whether gradient mass concentrates on easy or hard prompts), the sign axis (whether positive and negative masses are balanced, i.e., E[A]=0), and the scale axis (overall gradient magnitude, equivalent to implicit learning rate scaling). Three core trade-offs are formalized: 1) Amplifying correct trajectories collapses entropy, as clustered correct solutions push the policy into a narrow distribution; 2) Amplifying incorrect trajectories induces rank-1 update collapse, as diverse uncorrelated failures converge updates to a single suppression direction, blocking multi-dimensional learning; 3) Focusing on hard prompts increases update informativeness at the cost of higher variance. FADE (Focal Advantage with Dynamic Entropy) is proposed to dynamically adjust gradient weights based on the policy's historical entropy and solve rates, prioritizing exploration early in training (balanced sign weights, hard-problem focus) and sustaining diversity/accuracy in later stages.2d:T447,The method replaces monolithic autoregressive program generation with hierarchical generation. A decomposition policy first emits D, represented as function signatures plus natural-language docstrings. An implementation policy then samples implementations I_i for each function, modeled as π(D) and π(I_i | D). The key modeling assumption is conditional independence of module implementations given the decomposition. This can reduce coordination quality for any single sample, but it enables recombination. For n modules and k samples per module, the model spends GPU inference on only n×k module generations, while CPU-side validation can test up to k^n complete programs. The RL objective uses policy-gradient-style updates from binary verifier rewards on complete trajectories. The method is positioned as an exploration tool rather than a replacement for standard RL: standard RL is more sample-efficient when the initial policy already solves a nontrivial fraction of problems; DecompRL is meant for harder settings where the initial solve rate is nearly zero and verification is cheap.2e:T6b5,PMD organizes online experience into a three-level procedural memory and uses that memory only on the teacher path during training. The deployed student has no inference-time memory dependency. Level-0 experience memory stores raw trajectories, rewards, and feedback. It has the highest fidelity but weak transfer. Level-1 insight memory reflects over multiple attempts on the same problem and extracts compact strategies and lessons, removing much of the trajectory-level redundancy. Level-2 behavior memory groups semantically related problems and distills reusable reasoning patterns across them; this has the strongest transfer but also the highest risk of becoming too coarse. Training proceeds in a co-evolving loop: the current policy generates rollouts, receives verifier feedback, writes new information into memory, and the refreshed memory then conditions a self-teacher that supervises the next policy update. This is the main departure from SDPO. In SDPO, the teacher is still largely episode-local, built from sibling rollouts or local feedback. In PMD, the teacher is additionally informed by what the model has learned in prior epochs, so supervision carries cross-episode procedural structure. The co-evolution claim is central. A frozen memory becomes stale as the policy changes its visited states; a frozen policy prevents memory from improving in quality as capability grows. Another important design choice is to avoid inference-time memory use. Many reflection or memory-agent methods improve performance by carrying external memory into deployment, but that adds system complexity and a train-test mismatch. PMD instead treats memory as a training scaffold whose purpose is to be internalized.2f:T5cd,The headline results are fairly clear from the provided text. PMD is evaluated on two verifiable domains, SCIKNOWEVAL and LIVECODEBENCH, using Qwen3-8B and OLMo3-Instruct-7B as base models. Relative to GRPO and SDPO, PMD improves performance by 3.8–5.5% on SCIKNOWEVAL and 7.9–13.6% on LIVECODEBENCH. The most important evidence is mechanistic rather than just end-task gain: freezing either the memory while updating the policy, or freezing the policy while updating the memory, causes more than a 10% drop on SCIKNOWEVAL. That directly supports the claim that policy-memory co-evolution is the main source of improvement rather than an implementation detail. The paper also reports better test-time compute scaling: PMD continues to benefit from additional rollouts after SDPO starts to saturate, so the gap widens with rollout budget. That is consistent with the method's intent, since it aims to improve internalized procedural priors and teacher quality rather than only one-shot sampling success. The abstract also notes that combining Level-1 insight and Level-2 behavior memory gives the strongest distilled policy, suggesting that raw experience is too local while high-level behavior patterns alone are too coarse. The provided excerpt does not include deeper implementation details such as retrieval policy, reflection generation cost, token overhead of each memory level, or full stability curves, so those would need to be checked in the main experiments and appendix.30:T49d,Start with the method overview and Table 1, then move directly to the memory hierarchy and the ablations. On a first pass, focus on three questions. First, how exactly are teacher and student separated, and where does memory enter the teacher conditioning or supervision construction? Without that, it is easy to misread PMD as just another memory-augmented agent. Second, inspect the freezing ablations and the per-level memory ablations; these determine whether the paper truly establishes the necessity of co-evolution and the three-level design. Third, read the test-time rollout-scaling plots to see whether PMD improves single-sample quality, sample efficiency under larger budgets, or both. A skim should take about 30–40 minutes. For a deeper read, prioritize the memory write/read pipeline, the templates or procedures used to build insight and behavior memory, and whether the trends are consistent across both Qwen3-8B and OLMo3-Instruct-7B. The most interesting open question for post-training and RL-pretraining researchers is which kinds of procedural knowledge can actually be internalized into parameters and which still require explicit external search or tools.31:T46b,The authors construct an end-to-end mechanism around BPE fragmentation. Space insertion is used as a clean probe because it preserves all original characters while forcing BPE segmentation changes. The perturbation is optimized greedily to reduce a safety logit gap, rather than sampled randomly. The gap is measured at the first generated position: refusal tokens include I, Sorry, As, No, Unfortunately, and cannot, while compliance tokens include Sure, Okay, Here, and Step. A positive gap indicates a refusal tendency. The authors are careful to treat this as a first-token refusal trigger, not as a full behavioral safety metric. They then separate trigger flips from actual harmful generations. Activation patching is used to localize the disrupted signal, which appears mainly in the last roughly 30% of layers. A data audit of three public alignment datasets finds no intentionally fragmented inputs. Targeted mutation further isolates safety-critical words as the main locus of disruption. The defense study evaluates DPO and SFT-style augmentation and shows that neither gives a clean selective fix across model families.32:T4cc,The main experiments cover five model families: Qwen-3-4B, Qwen-2.5-7B, Gemma-3-4B, Llama-3.1-8B, and Mistral-7B, with additional cross-scale evidence from Qwen-2.5-72B. On refused HarmBench prompts, an optimization targeting safety-token fragmentation flips the first-token refusal trigger in 80–100% of cases. Among those trigger flips, 48% produce genuinely harmful outputs, with per-model rates from 29% to 65%. The gap-behavior ROC-AUC ranges from 0.66 to 0.98, with a pooled value of 0.84, indicating that the logit gap is informative but not sufficient. A Unicode no-split control is 2.2 times weaker, supporting the claim that the effect is tied to BPE segmentation rather than visual noise alone. Activation patching localizes the disrupted refusal signal to the last about 30% of layers. The alignment-data survey finds zero intentionally fragmented inputs in three datasets. In the defense experiments, no configuration in a 68-cell DPO grid closes ASR in a seed-stable way; a four-seed Mistral retraining run with 438 pairs still shows a 20–37% seed spread. SFT augmentation reduces ASR for 3 of 5 families, but it also increases refusal on benign prompts, which the authors expose with a Conv-Benign diagnostic.33:T7e9,The method has three layers. First, the authors train per-layer MLP probes on residual-stream hidden states to regress the current denoising progress τ during inference. They do this on two representative masked DLMs, LLaDA-1.5 and Dream, and vary which token positions are used: masked tokens only, non-masked tokens only, or all tokens. This is an important design choice because it tests whether the signal is localized to visibly masked positions or distributed more globally across the sequence representation.

Second, they move from readout to intervention. From the learned time-related representation, they identify a low-dimensional subspace associated with inferred denoising progress and steer activations along that subspace. The key point is that they do not alter the external unmasking schedule or the observed mask ratio; they only perturb internal states. If generation behavior shifts in a systematic way, that is stronger evidence that the model is using an internal notion of progress rather than merely exposing a correlational artifact.

Third, they analyze the geometry of the recovered signal across layers. The paper argues that denoising progress is encoded in a relatively low-dimensional subspace rather than as a diffuse weak statistic spread across the full hidden state. That matters because a compact control-like representation is easier to interpret as an internal state variable. The layerwise analysis also helps distinguish whether the signal is strongest in early, middle, or late computation and whether similar structure appears across different DLM families.

Relative to prior DLM interpretability work, this paper is narrower and more central: it does not focus on attention sinks, harmful mask manipulations, or generic SAE features, but on the internal representation of denoising progress itself. It also differs from theoretical work that links mask ratio to diffusion time analytically; here the question is whether the model actually instantiates such a variable internally.34:T6d3,The empirical study is built around LLaDA-1.5 and Dream. The main takeaways visible from the full text are:

1. Probe results: on LLaDA, layerwise probes recover τ with R² above 0.5 over a substantial range of layers, indicating that denoising progress is not only weakly present or confined to a single stage. The token-subset comparisons are especially important. If non-masked tokens also carry strong τ information, that supports the interpretation of a global latent state rather than a trivial reflection of visible mask positions.

2. Steering results: perturbing activations along the recovered time subspace systematically modulates the model’s effective notion of denoising stage. The abstract states this qualitatively; the main text should be checked for the exact quantitative readouts used to support the claim, such as changes in unmasking behavior, entropy, confidence, or output characteristics. The key thing to verify is whether the effect is monotonic and robust across layers and prompts.

3. Geometric analysis: the paper argues that the time signal lives in a low-dimensional subspace and studies how that structure evolves across depth. The most useful details to extract while reading are: how low-dimensional the signal is, whether the dominant directions align across layers, whether there is a middle-layer peak, and whether LLaDA and Dream show similar patterns.

From a pretraining-research perspective, the strongest part of the experimental package is the progression from decodability to causal steering to representational geometry. The main limitation is that the paper stops at analysis; it does not yet turn the discovered latent clock into a better training objective or a more efficient decoding policy.35:T573,Start with the definition of τ and the three research questions in the introduction. Then read the probing section carefully, especially the setup behind Figure 2. That is the foundation of the paper. Pay attention to whether the authors rule out trivial shortcuts such as direct dependence on visible mask statistics, token identity, or positional artifacts.

Next, focus on the steering section. The important questions are how the time subspace is constructed, whether intervention is applied at one layer or multiple layers, and whether the perturbation is a simple additive shift or something more structured. This determines how strong the causal claim really is.

Then read the geometry section and the appendix. The appendix is likely where the probe architecture, training details, token-subset definitions, and the relation between τ and the continuous corruption variable s are spelled out. For a quick pass, the main figures on probing, steering, and layerwise geometry are enough in about 20–30 minutes. For a serious mechanistic read, including appendices, budget around an hour.

Two follow-up questions are worth keeping in mind while reading. First, can this latent clock be used for adaptive decoding, where the model decides how aggressively to unmask? Second, is the recovered signal only a proxy for mask ratio, or does it encode a richer notion of residual uncertainty?36:T43e,Proposes Expander SAEs, tied-weight TopK SAEs where both encoder and decoder weights are constrained by a fixed adjacency mask of a left d-regular bipartite expander graph (d ≪ activation dimension m). This reduces learnable decoder parameters from O(mn) to O(dn) while preserving the full sparse coding problem dimensions (m, n, k). Core contributions include: 1) an architecture that decouples dictionary quality from amortized inference quality; 2) a parallel orthogonal matching pursuit (OMP) implementation optimized for the column-sparse structure, delivering a Pareto frontier between fast amortized encoding and high-fidelity iterative decoding; 3) theoretical proofs that k-sparse codes are uniquely identifiable under 2k-expansion and decoder column flatness conditions, with exact support recovery guarantees for OMP under a cumulative coherence condition. Unlike prior SAE variants (Gated, JumpReLU, TopK) that only modify encoder sparsity mechanisms with dense decoders, this is the first SAE to use fixed decoder column sparsity as a 解决的是预训练数据 mixture 中“标签系统”不够灵活的问题。很多数据混合方法默认语料已经被分好组，但分组方式决定了 mixer 能表达什么。来源标签太粗，人工 taxonomy 只能沿一个语义轴划分，普通 embedding clustering 又是固定 K 的平面划分，想换粒度就要重新聚类。HERMES 把问题转到一次性、多粒度、层级化的数据标注：每篇文档只标一次，之后通过 code prefix 改变粒度。","importance":"这是一篇预训练数据方向的工作，贡献点在 label substrate，而不是新的 sampler。它的实际意义在于把“数据分组”从单次工程选择变成可调旋钮。对大规模预训练来说，这很关键：RegMix、Data Mixing Laws 这类回归式 mixer 在十万级 cell 上代价很高；逐文档 selector 又需要 web-scale per-document compute。HERMES 处在中间位置，用离线 embedding 和层级 RVQ 给约 5000 万文档打标签，支持从 256 个 coarse buckets 到约 6.5 万、约 13 万 observed cells 的粒度扫描。更重要的是，它展示了 granularity 与 Stage-2 sampler 会相互作用：在 L12 粒度下质量筛选有效，在 L123 粒度下同样思路几乎失效。这对数据配比研究很有提醒意义，不能把“更细粒度分组”和“更强质量过滤”简单当作单调收益。","technical_approach":"HERMES 的输入是冻结 encoder 产生的文档 embedding，维度 d=1024，规模约 N=5×10^7。系统由两部分组成。第一部分是 Learned Semantic Transform，形式是 linear-plus-normalize：h_i = normalize(W e_i + b)，W 从 identity 初始化。它的目标不是学习新的语义模型，而是把 embedding 旋转到更适合量化的空间。训练时使用三类约束：pairwise structure preservation 保持原 embedding 的局部/全局关系，quantization-aware reconstruction 让变换后的表示更容易被 codebook 重构，orthogonality 约束避免线性变换退化。第二部分是 3-stage residual vector quantization，每一层 codebook 大小 K=256。第一层给出 c1，第二、三层依次量化残差，得到 (c1, c2, c3)。prefix 就是粒度控制：L1=c1 对应 256 个 buckets；L12=(c1,c2) 对应约 6.5 万 buckets；L123=(c1,c2,c3) 在实际语料中观察到约 13 万 cells。由于所有粒度来自同一套 codebook，比较不同 sampler 时不会混入“重新聚类导致分组变化”的因素。训练和采样上，外层权重只在 L1 级别使用 Uniform 或 DoReMi，避免在十万级 cell 上拟合高维 mixer。Stage-2 在每个 coarse bucket 内决定如何取文档：一种是 max-entropy coverage，尽量让 sub-bucket mass 更均匀并保留全部文档；另一种是 quality top-30%，按质量 reader 在候选内取前 30%，质量 reader 使用修正后的 FineWeb-Edu reader。论文还设计了 quota-preserving 的 L123 对照，用 per-L1 local random coverage 对比 L123 quality top-30%，把外层 quota 固定住，隔离细粒度下候选竞争的影响。","experiments":"实验规模是 1B 参数量模型、25B token 训练预算，评测使用 16 个任务的 capability macro-mean Avg.。首先，HERMES 并不声称自己在 K=256 上是更好的聚类算法。Table 2 显示它在 compactness 和 mass-balance 等标准聚类指标上与四种 256-way clusterer 处在同一平台；下游训练结果也与 KMeans 几乎持平，Avg. 差距在 0.0002 内。关键实验是 sampler 与粒度的受控比较。Finding A：在固定 corpus、model、codebook、outer weight 和 L12 粒度下，DoReMi-L1 外层权重不变，把 Stage-2 从 max-entropy coverage 切到 quality top-30%，16-task Avg. 提升 +0.0253，z=+2.09。这说明在 L12 的候选池大小下，质量 reader 的排序信号仍能转化成预训练收益。Finding B：同样的优势在 L123 粒度下消失。用 quota-preserving 对照比较 per-L1 L123 local random coverage 与 L123 quality top-30%，Avg. 分别为 0.3986 和 0.3988，差值只有 +0.0002，单 seed 下可视为数值持平。论文给出的诊断是 candidate competition：从 L12 到 L123，median candidate pool 从 2271 缩到 429，缩小 5.3 倍；当每个细粒度 cell 的候选太少时，局部 top-30% 排序不再稳定代表全局质量排序。这个结果比单纯报告某个 mixture 最优更有价值，因为它解释了为什么质量过滤在不同标签粒度下会出现不同收益。","reading_guide":"建议精读。先看 Introduction，抓住它把问题拆成 label system 与 mixer HERMES 的目标不是赢聚类指标，而是提供同一 annotation 下的粒度旋钮。Section 5.2 是主要正结果，关注 L12 下 Stage-2 sampler 的受控比较。Section 5.3 是更有研究价值的部分，说明细粒度并不自动增强质量筛选，candidate pool size 会改变 sampler 的有效性。若做数据配比或数据选择，建议继续看 Appendix L 中 Uniform 与 DoReMi-L1 的配对分析，判断外层 mixture weight 与内层文档选择是否有耦合。需要注意单 seed 训练会限制统计强度，但实验控制做得比较清楚，结论适合转化为后续大规模 ablation 的设计原则。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"HERMES targets the labeling bottleneck in pretraining data mixtures. Most mixing methods assume that the corpus has already been partitioned, but the partition itself limits what the mixer can express. Provenance labels are coarse, hand-built taxonomies fix one semantic axis, and flat embedding clusters must be recomputed when the desired resolution changes. HERMES provides a one-shot hierarchical labeling substrate: each document receives a coarse-to-fine code once, and the prefix length controls the mixture granularity.","importance":"The main contribution is a data-labeling substrate for pretraining, not a new mixture optimizer. This distinction matters. Regression-style mixture optimizers become costly when the number of cells grows to tens or hundreds of thousands, while per-document selectors require expensive compute at web scale. HERMES occupies a practical middle ground: offline annotation over roughly 50 million documents, with a usable granularity dial from 256 coarse buckets to about 65k and 130k observed cells. The most informative result is not that HERMES beats clustering at a fixed K. It does not claim that. The useful finding is that granularity and the within-bucket sampler interact. Quality filtering helps at the L12 level, but the same idea nearly vanishes at L123 because candidate pools become too small. This is directly relevant to data-mixture research: finer labels and stronger filtering do not guarantee monotonic gains.","technical_approach":"$16","experiments":"$17","reading_guide":"Read it carefully if working on pretraining data mixtures. Then read the method section on the Learned Semantic Transform and residual vector quantization, focusing on why a rotate-then-quantize pipeline is preferred over direct flat clustering or a fixed taxonomy. Appendix D is worth checking for the exact losses and training recipe. Section 5.1 and Table 2 should be read as sanity checks: HERMES is not trying to win a fixed-K clustering contest. global","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-02T04:27:45Z","category_id":"pretraining_data","cover_url":"/covers/2607_01686.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文研究如何只从 released weights 中恢复一个 fine-tuned model 的训练数据领域配比。它不是做单样本 membership inference，而是估计训练语料在不同 domain 上的全局 mixture，例如 web、code、science 或若干已知文本集合的采样比例。输入假设是有 base model、fine-tuned model，以及可从候选数据源中采样少量 probe examples。","importance":"对预训练和持续训练研究的启发在于，它把数据配比从一个只能由训练方记录的 recipe，转化为可从权重几何中反推的对象。这个问题对模型审计、数据污染分析、continual pretrain 的分布漂移诊断都有意义。贡献更偏测量和反演方法，不是提出新的 data mixture 优化算法。它的限制也明确：实验主要是 controlled fine-tuning，模型是 BERT 和 GPT-2 级别；方法依赖已知候选数据源和 probe samples。若用于真实大模型预训练配方恢复，还需要处理更复杂的 domain taxonomy、长训练轨迹、RL/SFT 多阶段混合和架构差异。","technical_approach":"WARP 的思路是利用 base weights 和 fine-tuned weights 之间的 weight-space geometry。真实训练轨迹通常不可见，作者用 model merging / interpolation 在 base 与 fine-tuned endpoint 之间构造 pseudo-checkpoints，把它们当作缺失轨迹的替代。随后用来自候选 domain 的 probe examples 计算几何特征，核心是衡量不同 domain 样本与这些权重空间方向的对齐关系。读出方式有两类：一种是 parameter-free softmax readout，用于粗粒度 mixture recovery；另一种是用 synthetic mixtures 训练 MLP，把几何特征映射到 mixture prediction。与 membership inference 相比，WARP 不判断某个样本是否出现，而是聚合 domain-level signal；与需要真实 checkpoint trajectory 的权重分析方法相比，它只需要 base 和 final weights。","experiments":"作者在 controlled setting 中训练 BERT 和 GPT-2，使用已知 mixture 的四个文本数据集，随后从 fine-tuned checkpoint 反推配比。总计 40 个实验 trial。WARP 在 BERT 上的 mean absolute error 最低为 0.046，在 GPT-2 上为 0.104，均为四个文本数据集上的平均结果。它优于 sample-level membership inference baselines，也优于一个可以访问真实训练轨迹的变体。论文还测试了 early-stop、converged 和 overtrained checkpoints，结果显示估计误差对训练停止点不敏感，这一点贴近当前 post-training 中经常过训练或延长训练预算的情况。","reading_guide":"建议泛读 20 到 30 分钟。先看 Introduction 对问题边界的定义，尤其是 domain mixture recovery 与 membership inference 的区别。Framework limitations：候选数据源是否已知、probe data 是否代表训练域、fine-tuning 与 pretraining 的尺度差距是否会破坏几何信号。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks whether one can recover the domain mixture used to fine-tune a released model using only its weights. The target is not sample-level membership inference, but a global estimate of how much each candidate domain contributed to training. The assumed inputs are a base model, its fine-tuned descendant, and a small probing set sampled from the candidate data sources.","importance":"The relevance to pretraining and continual training is that data mixture becomes an inferable property of weights, rather than only a private training recipe. This is useful for model auditing, contamination analysis, and diagnosing distribution drift during continual pretraining or repeated fine-tuning. The contribution is a measurement and inverse-analysis method, not a new mixture optimization algorithm. Its current evidence is in controlled fine-tuning with BERT and GPT-2, and it assumes access to candidate data sources. Applying it to real large-model pretraining would require handling richer domain taxonomies, multi-stage SFT/RL pipelines, longer trajectories, and architecture changes.","technical_approach":"WARP uses the geometry between base and fine-tuned weights. Since the true training trajectory is usually unavailable, it constructs pseudo-checkpoints through model merging or interpolation between the two endpoints. These pseudo-trajectories are then used to extract geometric features from probe examples sampled from each candidate domain. The features are designed to capture how domain-specific samples align with directions in weight space. The readout has two variants: a parameter-free softmax estimator for coarse mixture recovery, and an MLP trained on synthetic mixtures to learn a stronger mapping from geometric features to mixture proportions. Compared with membership inference, WARP aggregates domain-level signals instead of detecting individual training samples. Compared with trajectory-based weight-space analyses, it only needs the base and final checkpoints.","experiments":"The empirical setup trains BERT and GPT-2 under known mixtures over four text datasets, then attempts to recover the mixture from each fine-tuned checkpoint. Across 40 trials, WARP reaches a mean absolute error as low as 0.046 on BERT and 0.104 on GPT-2, averaged across the four domains. It outperforms sample-level membership inference baselines and even a variant that has access to the true training trajectory. The paper also evaluates early-stopped, converged, and overtrained checkpoints, showing that the estimator remains relatively stable across different stopping points, which is relevant to modern post-training regimes where models are often trained beyond compute-optimal budgets.","reading_guide":"A useful skim takes about 20 to 30 minutes. Start with the problem formulation in the Introduction, especially the distinction between domain mixture recovery and membership inference. The Framework section is the core: read Figure 2, the pseudo-checkpoint construction, the geometric feature extraction, and the two readout mechanisms. In the experiments, focus on the BERT/GPT-2 controlled runs, the MAE tables, comparisons with MI baselines, and robustness across early-stop, converged, and overtrained checkpoints. For large-model relevance, pay close attention to the assumptions: whether the candidate data source is known, whether probe data represents the training domains, and whether signals from fine-tuning-scale experiments survive at pretraining scale.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"从权重空间反推训练数据配比的思路新颖，对分析闭源模型训练配方有实用价值，重点看模型插值生成伪检查点的设计。","en":"Novel framework for recovering training data mixture from model weights, useful for analyzing closed-source model recipes; focus on the pseudo-checkpoint interpolation design."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Tzu-Heng Huang","Aditya Goyal","John Cooper","Frederic Sala"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["training data inference","weight space analysis","model merging","membership inference"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01686","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.01686","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2607.01601","title":"SemHash-LLM: A Multi-Granularity Semantic Hashing Framework for Document Deduplication","abstract":"Large scale document deduplication must preserve semantic equivalence while remaining efficient over massive corpora. We present SemHash LLM, a multi granularity framework that unifies semantic projection hashing, attention weighted MinHash, contrastive boundary learning, and selective LLM based adjudication. The method combines character, token, and document level signals through gated fusion, then applies a cascaded filtering pipeline for efficient candidate reduction. We introduce Ember, a lightweight optimizer for embedding and LM-head matrices that utilizes O(V + D) VRAM, ","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-01T20:21:03Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2607_01455.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"Transformer的输入嵌入表和输出LM头（统称token接口参数）目前普遍使用Adam优化，显存开销为O(2VD)，需要复杂的分布式分片工程。现有主流优化器改进（如Muon）几乎都仅针对稠密线性层，未覆盖token接口参数，也未利用其与交叉熵损失绑定的独特梯度几何结构（Fisher信息度量），导致显存浪费与工程冗余。","importance":"对预训练研究的具体影响分为两部分：一是工程层面，提出的Ember优化器将token接口的优化器显存开销从O(2VD)降至O(V+D)，典型大词表场景下仅需KB级状态，完全消除了嵌入表优化器状态的分片需求，可直接嵌入现有预训练、SFT、RL全流程，在不损失精度的前提下优化训练Pareto边界。二是理论层面，统一了Adam、Muon、Lion等主流Transformer优化器的底层逻辑——本质都是利用Fisher度量的平方根对梯度做归一化（等价于将梯度转换为z-score），为后续优化器研究提供了一致的数学框架。","technical_approach":"首先建立统一理论视角：交叉熵损失的二阶导数恰好等于梯度的平方（即Fisher信息度量，也是KL散度唯一的规范局部几何结构），现有高性能Transformer优化器均等价于用Fisher度量的平方根对梯度做条件缩放，对应除以梯度估计的标准差。基于此设计Ember优化器，核心有两点：1. 完全移除Adam的一阶矩指数移动平均（EMA），直接使用瞬时梯度，消融实验证明该改动反而提升性能，同时节省V×D的显存；2. 观察到token接口参数的行梯度能量是token出现概率p_i的无偏估计，且行级Fisher与p_i²成正比，因此无需存储全量V×D的二阶矩缓冲，仅需维护V个行级与D个列级的二阶矩EMA即可近似曲率。与同类方法的区别：Adafactor虽也做二阶矩分解，但超参更多且未针对token接口的几何特性设计；Muon仅适用于线性层，依赖奇异值分解，无法直接用于嵌入表。","experiments":"实验验证了三个核心结论：1. Most recent optimizer improvements (e.g., Muon) only target dense linear layers, leaving token interfaces unaddressed and failing to exploit their unique gradient geometry tied to the Fisher information structure of cross-entropy loss, resulting in wasted memory and redundant engineering work.","importance":"The work delivers two layers of value for pretraining research: On the engineering side, the proposed Ember optimizer reduces token interface optimizer VRAM from O(2VD) to O(V+D) — kilobyte-scale state for typical large vocabularies — eliminating sharding requirements for embedding/LM head states and directly fitting into existing pretraining, SFT, and RL pipelines while improving the accuracy-memory Pareto frontier with no performance tradeoff. Existing fault-tolerance mechanisms either impose non-trivial overhead during failure-free execution or suffer from prolonged recovery latency, particularly under scenarios where a small subset of compute nodes experience permanent failures. %The tradeoff between failure-free overhead and recovery latency forms","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-02T03:17:58Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2607_01646.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.DC"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这项工作解决大规模 LLM 训练中的节点永久故障恢复问题：既要避免正常训练时频繁 checkpoint 的开销，又要避免故障后整作业重启、排队和回滚重算。","importance":"这是训练系统方向的工作，对预训练研究的价值在工程可靠性。万卡级训练中，GPU HBM 错误、PCIe 故障、NCCL watchdog timeout 等会成为常态事件；如果恢复机制依赖持久化 checkpoint 和整作业重启，损失的不只是 I/O 时间，还包括队列等待、拓扑重建和上一次 checkpoint 之后的训练步数。DeadPool 的贡献是把故障恢复从“重启作业”改成“在线拓扑修复”，并把每步 optimizer state 备份放到训练关键路径之外。对长周期预训练尤其有意义，因为它降低了故障尾部风险，而不是只优化单步吞吐。","technical_approach":"DeadPool 基于两个设计点。第一，在 3D parallelism 中区分哪些状态可重构、哪些状态不可重构。模型参数分片通常在 data-parallel peers 中有副本，节点坏掉后可以从健康 rank 恢复；但 sharded optimizer states，尤其是一阶、二阶动量，被切在 data-parallel rank 内部，永久节点故障会直接丢失。DeadPool 因此设计跨节点的 in-memory optimizer state replication，每个 iteration 复制优化器分片，并通过异步传输和计算重叠来避免暴露开销。第二，它把故障处理建模为在线 topology repair：用预留 spare node 替换失败节点，重建通信组，而不是终止整个训练作业。系统还包含错误分类层，区分 transient failure 和 permanent failure，只对真正破坏成员关系的故障触发热替换。实现基于 PyTorch 和 Megatron-LM，但依赖的是通用机制：sharded model/optimizer states、spare-node 管理、恢复协调和 runtime communicator reconstruction。","experiments":"实验在两个生产 HPC 系统上完成：NERSC Perlmutter 使用最高 512 张 A100，TACC Vista 使用 64 张 GH200。评测包含 live training 中注入节点故障，并预留 spare nodes。模型规模最高到 65B。结果显示，正常训练阶段每步 in-memory checkpoint 对关键路径的额外开销可以忽略；故障发生后，DeadPool 通常在约 40 秒内完成节点替换并恢复训练。论文还指出 zero-overhead 依赖集群配置、模型大小和并行切分方式；但 Figure 6 中 checkpoint 通信量相对计算开销低一个数量级，这是它能隐藏备份成本的主要条件。","reading_guide":"泛读建议 25–35 分钟。先看 Introduction 中对传统 checkpoint-restart、in-memory checkpoint 和 elastic fault tolerance 的取舍比较。然后重点看系统设计中 optimizer state 为什么不可从 DP 副本恢复，以及 neighbor-memory replication 如何和计算重叠。再看 communicator reconstruction 和错误分类，因为这决定了方案能否从论文原型变成可运维系统。实验部分优先看 512 A100、64 GH200、65B 模型设置，以及 Figure 6 的通信/计算重叠分析。若关心自家训练集群落地，重点检查 spare node 预留成本、NCCL/通信组重建限制、以及不同 ZeRO/Megatron 切分配置下 optimizer state 的副本策略。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses permanent node failures during large-scale LLM training. The goal is to avoid both steady-state checkpoint overhead and expensive full-job restart after a failure.","importance":"This is a training-systems paper with direct relevance to long-running pretraining jobs. At thousands to tens of thousands of GPUs, failures such as HBM faults, PCIe errors, and NCCL watchdog timeouts are operational events rather than rare exceptions. Traditional checkpoint-restart loses time through filesystem I/O, recomputation since the last checkpoint, global job teardown, and sometimes queueing delay. DeadPool reframes recovery as online topology repair and backs up the state that cannot be reconstructed from existing replicas. The value is reliability over long training horizons, not a change in model quality or optimization objective.","technical_approach":"$1a","experiments":"The evaluation runs on two production HPC systems: up to 512 A100 GPUs on NERSC Perlmutter and 64 GH200 GPUs on TACC Vista. The authors inject node failures into live training jobs with reserved spare nodes, and evaluate models up to 65B parameters. The reported steady-state checkpoint overhead is negligible on the training critical path. After a failure, DeadPool completes node replacement and resumes training in roughly 40 seconds. The zero-overhead claim is conditional on cluster configuration, model size, and parallelization strategy. A key empirical reason is that the checkpointing communication shown in Figure 6 is about an order of magnitude smaller than the compute window it overlaps with.","reading_guide":"A 25–35 minute skim is sufficient. The communicator reconstruction protocol and failure-classification layer are important for judging operational feasibility. In the evaluation, focus on the 512-A100, 64-GH200, and 65B-model settings, plus Figure 6 for the communication-versus-compute overlap. in","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-02T04:10:42Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2607_01678.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.DC"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文解决的是大规模 的核心做法是稀疏化优化器状态，而不是直接稀疏化当前 step 的原始梯度。作者基于 AdamS，而不是 AdamW。全文中的关键观察有两个：第一，在高稀疏通信下，AdamS 的残差增长比 AdamW 小，说明它对 error-feedback 中的 stale residual 更稳；第二，相邻训练 step 的一阶动量 top-k 结构有时间稳定性，因此 mask 可以延迟一个 step 使用。基于这两个观察，SCAPE 设计了 partitioned mask-refresh：每个分片根据动量统计生成稀疏 mask，mask 的同步与计算重叠，下一步再使用，避免把 mask 生成和通信放在 critical path 上。它还用一个同步后的 sparse buffer 重构二阶矩更新所需的量，避免额外的第二次 all-reduce。在 sharded data parallel pretraining, especially gradient synchronization and parameter reconstruction under data parallel and sharded training schemes. The goal is not to reduce FLOPs, but to cut communication volume aggressively while preserving convergence and downstream quality. Existing top-k methods are brittle when applied to raw gradients with Adam-style optimizers, while quantization is bounded by bit width and can introduce nontrivial runtime overhead. SCAPE is designed to push sparsity much further without those failure modes.","importance":"The main relevance is at the optimizer-system boundary for pretraining. The useful lesson is that communication sparsification does not have to operate on raw gradients; optimizer moments can provide a more stable signal for mask construction. This matters for large training runs because all-reduce, reduce-scatter, and all-gather become increasingly dominant as model and cluster sizes grow. The contribution is a practical distributed optimizer design rather than a new modeling objective or architecture. The evidence is still limited to GPT-345M and Llama-500M, so extrapolation to multi-billion or frontier-scale runs should be cautious. The implementation also benefits from GH200 CPU-GPU bandwidth for error-feedback buffer offload, which may not transfer cleanly to PCIe-based clusters.","technical_approach":"SCAPE sparsifies optimizer-state-derived quantities instead of raw gradients. It builds on AdamS rather than AdamW. Two observations drive the design. First, under high sparsity, AdamS shows much smaller residual growth than AdamW, suggesting better robustness to stale error-feedback dynamics. Second, the top-k structure of the first moment is stable across adjacent steps, making one-step delayed mask reuse practical. SCAPE therefore introduces a partitioned mask-refresh mechanism aligned with optimizer sharding. Masks are computed from momentum statistics, synchronized while computation proceeds, and used in the next step. The method also reconstructs the quantities needed for second-moment updates from a single synchronized sparse buffer, avoiding an additional all-reduce. In sharded data parallel training, the design compresses both reduce-scatter for gradient synchronization and all-gather for parameter reconstruction.","experiments":"The method is implemented in Megatron-LM and evaluated on the TACC Vista GH200 system. The paper pretrains GPT-345M on OpenWebText and Llama-500M on SlimPajama-6B. The headline result is for Llama-500M on 32 GH200 GPUs: 90% sparsity reduces wall-clock training time by 35.6%, and 99% sparsity reduces it by 43.3%. Final training and validation losses remain close to dense AdamS and AdamW. Downstream evaluation includes LAMBADA, SuperGLUE, PIQA, MMLU, and ARC, with no reported quality degradation. The experimental story also compares against raw-gradient sparsification and quantization-style methods, arguing that moment-based sparsity is more stable at extreme sparsity and that sparsity is not limited by a fixed bit-width floor.","reading_guide":"A useful skim takes about 25 to 35 minutes. Moreover, because it makes use of training runs with suboptimal batch size, our proposed law can be robustly fit with a significantly smaller amount of training runs. We further show that the ","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-01T21:32:14Z","category_id":"scaling","cover_url":"/covers/2607_01487.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","stat.ML"],"summary":{"core_problem":"经典 Chinchilla 类 scaling law 把训练数据压成一个总 token 数 D，默认 batch size 等超参数已经调到合适位置。这个设定无法回答一个实际问题：在同样 token 预算下，应该用更大的 batch、还是更多训练 step。该工作把数据预算显式拆成 batch size M 和训练步数 K，建模最终 loss 随 N、M、K 的变化。","importance":"对预训练规划有直接启发。大规模训练里，batch size 不只是系统吞吐参数，也会影响固定 token 预算下的优化轨迹和最终 loss。该工作给出一个可以同时预测最优 batch、次优 batch 损失代价、以及 Chinchilla 式 N-D 最优分配的统一形式。贡献主要是 scaling-law 方法论：它不提出新模型，但能减少 batch sweep 成本，并让 suboptimal batch 的训练结果也参与拟合，这一点对真实预训练实验很有用。","technical_approach":"核心是三项 scaling law：把 loss 写成模型规模项、训练步数项、batch size 项的加和形式，可理解为 L(N,M,K)=E + A N^{-α} + B K^{-β} + C M^{-γ} 这一类结构，并保持 D=MK。K 项刻画更新步数带来的优化收益，M 项刻画 batch 增大后梯度噪声降低带来的收益，但在固定 D 下，增大 M 会减少 K，因此自然产生非平凡最优 batch。和直接拟合“最优 batch size 是 D 的幂律”不同，它拟合的是 loss 曲面，所以 suboptimal batch 的 run 也有信息量。和传统 critical batch size 公式相比，它不会推出最优 batch 为 1，并且能在 AdamW 训练下恢复经验上观察到的 batch 随 token budget 增长的规律。","experiments":"实验使用两组 dense LLM 训练数据：Li et al. 2025a 的训练 run，以及 OpenEuroLLM 的训练 run。两组数据都覆盖多个模型规模、token budget 和 batch size。关键结果是：三项 law 能恢复已有工作中最优 batch size 随 D 的幂律增长；每个 (N,D) 只取两个 batch size 就能稳健拟合 batch scaling，所需训练 run 数约为完整 sweep 的 28%；在 out-of-sample token budget 上，ε-suboptimal batch size 的预测也能泛化。它还给出 critical batch size 随 N 或 D 的变化关系，和 Zhang et al. 2025 的经验发现一致；最优 batch size 对模型规模不敏感，这一点也符合 Li et al. 2025a 的结果。","reading_guide":"泛读 25 分钟即可。重点看 Overview 和 Scaling Laws with Training Steps and Batch Size，确认三项 loss 形式如何推出最优 batch 幂律；再看 Section 4.1 和 4.2，那里是“用少量 suboptimal batch run 恢复 batch scaling”的主要证据；Section 4.3 的 ε-suboptimal batch 对硬件受限训练更实用；Section 4.4 适合和 critical batch size 文献对照。读时要特别检查拟合协议、外推区间、残差图，以及不同数据集是否共享同一训练 recipe，因为这些因素会决定 scaling law 能否迁移到自己的预训练设置。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"Standard Chinchilla-style scaling laws collapse training data into a single token count D and implicitly assume that hyperparameters such as batch size are already near-optimal. This leaves a practical planning question unresolved: under a fixed token budget, should one spend tokens on more optimization steps or on a larger batch? The paper models final loss as a function of model size N, batch size M, and number of training steps K, with D=MK.","importance":"The main value is in pretraining experiment design. Batch size is not merely a throughput knob; under a fixed token budget it changes the number of parameter updates and therefore the final loss. The proposed law gives a single framework for predicting optimal batch size, estimating the penalty of using a hardware-constrained suboptimal batch, and recovering a Chinchilla-like N-D law after optimizing over M. Its contribution is methodological rather than architectural, but it is useful because it turns otherwise imperfect batch-size runs into data for fitting the law.","technical_approach":"The paper introduces a three-term loss law over N, M, and K, conceptually of the form L(N,M,K)=E + A N^{-α} + B K^{-β} + C M^{-γ}. The K term represents the benefit of more optimizer updates, while the M term captures the benefit of larger batches through reduced stochasticity. Since D=MK, increasing M at fixed D reduces K, which yields a non-trivial optimal batch size. This differs from works that directly fit a power law for the optimal batch size, because here the whole loss surface is modeled, including suboptimal batches. It also avoids the degeneracy of earlier critical-batch formulations that imply an optimal batch size of one.","experiments":"The law is fitted on two collections of dense LLM training runs: the dataset from Li et al. 2025a and an OpenEuroLLM training dataset. Both contain variation over model size, token budget, and batch size. The fitted law recovers previously reported optimal batch-size scaling. A notable result is that two batch sizes per (N,D) are enough to recover the batch law robustly, reducing the number of required training runs to about 28% of a full sweep. The paper also derives ε-suboptimal batch-size laws that generalize to held-out token budgets, and its induced critical-batch behavior agrees with empirical findings such as Zhang et al. 2025.","reading_guide":"A 25-minute skim is enough for most readers. Focus on the overview and the section deriving the N-M-K law, then read Sections 4.1 and 4.2 for the evidence that suboptimal batch runs can recover the optimal batch-size law. Section 4.3 is the most practically relevant part for hardware-constrained training. Section 4.4 is useful if you care about the connection to critical batch size. API。像日志告警、脏文本解析、模糊匹配、意图排序、JSON 修复这类任务，规则系统太脆，直接调大模型又贵、不可复现、难本地部署。论文提出把“函数规格”编译成一个可本地执行的神经程序，让模糊函数像普通软件模块一样被保存、分发和调用。核心难点有三个：一是如何把自然语言规格稳定地转成可执行行为，而不是只靠一次性 prompt；二是如何让很小的解释器承接复杂任务；三是如何构造足够大、足够多样的训练数据，让“编译器”学会从规格到参数高效映射。","importance":"对预训练研究的意义主要在范式层面，而不只是一个应用系统。它把“模型参数”从通用能力载体，进一步变成“程序表示”，把一次性推理开销前移到编译阶段。这和传统 AR 语言模型每次都在线推理不同，更接近“预训练基座 + 任务编译 + 不只是适配工具，也可以作为程序中间表示。第二，数据集不再只是训练通用模型，也可以训练“从规格到权重”的编译器。第三，离线编译与在线执行分离后，模型能力、部署成本、可复现性之间出现了新的权衡空间。这类思路和 hypernetwork、weight generation、in-context learning
